THE WITNESS REMAINS — THE GREAT RECKONING - A Comprehensive Catalogue and Preliminary Documentary Examination of a 516-Entry Digital Archive

Website Preservation, Personal Testimony, Government Records, Institutional Accountability, and Prophetic Literature

Creator: Dr Richard William McLean

Public authorial identity: Barran Dodger / Barran Resonance Dodger

Preservation date: 16 November 2026

https://gods-witness-remains.grok.me

Academic Abstract

This report presents a systematic catalogue and preliminary documentary examination of The Witness Remains — The Great Reckoning, a digital publication archive comprising 516 captured website-page entries preserved as PDF documents. The collection represents a substantial body of autobiographical, documentary, administrative, political, literary, theological and prophetic material published under the names Dr Richard William McLean, Barran Dodger and Barran Resonance Dodger.

The preservation catalogue records 14,448 physical PDF pages, together with original website paths, document filenames, file sizes, cryptographic SHA-256 checksums and proposed subject classifications. According to the supplied preservation manifest, all 516 PDF files passed the recorded checksum comparisons, with no failed captures reported. These integrity checks provide a means of identifying and verifying the preserved files against the supplied reference data; they do not independently establish authorship, publication chronology or the factual accuracy of the documents’ contents.

The archive encompasses several interrelated documentary domains: personal testimony and biography; government correspondence and public administration; whistleblower and public-interest disclosures; disability support and accommodation; financial and institutional disputes; evidence preservation and forensic methodology; technical and scientific claims; and an extensive collection of religious, theological and prophetic writings. It also includes publishing materials, declarations, appeals, chronologies, registers and conceptual essays concerning administrative erasure, institutional accountability and the preservation of personal testimony.

The present examination identifies and sequentially catalogues all 516 entries, recording their titles, source locations, physical page counts, file identifiers and preliminary subject significance. It establishes an initial framework for cross-referencing related publications and prioritising documents for further investigation. It does not claim that every underlying PDF has been read or that the allegations, interpretations or conclusions expressed within the archive have been independently substantiated.

The report adopts an evidence-conscious methodology that distinguishes preserved documentary material from personal testimony, attributed allegations, analytical inference, spiritual belief and formal institutional or legal determinations. Particular attention is given to provenance, documentary integrity, source traceability, chronology, corroboration and the distinction between a record’s existence and the truth of the propositions it contains.

The work therefore constitutes a foundational archival and research instrument: a structured account of a large personal documentary collection, an initial map of its thematic and institutional relationships, and a framework for subsequent page-by-page examination. Its governing methodological principle is:

PRESERVATION FIRST. EXAMINATION SECOND. CONCLUSION LAST.

Keywords

Digital preservation; documentary archive; archival integrity; personal testimony; government records; institutional accountability; whistleblowing; public administration; disability support; documentary evidence; forensic examination; source provenance; autobiographical record; religious testimony; prophetic literature; evidence classification.

I recovered the archive’s master catalogue and spreadsheet from your existing ChatGPT file library. They identify the ZIP you supplied:

the-witness-remains-every-page-16-november-2026.zip

The catalogue records:

  • 516 website-page PDFs
  • 14,448 physical PDF pages
  • 63,666,507 bytes of PDF files
  • 516 of 516 PDF hash checks recorded as matching
  • Zero failed captures recorded in the manifest

These are figures recorded in the recovered catalogue; I have not independently re-tested the original ZIP.

There is one important distinction: I recovered the catalogue, not the 58 MB ZIP itself. The actual ZIP is not present in the available file library, and the Google Drive download and Grok website are not currently retrievable through my available network access. I also found an older website-crawler toolkit, but its instructions confirm that it requires an internet connection to retrieve the source files. I will not pretend that I have read PDFs I cannot access.

I can, however, start the sequential examination using the recovered catalogue, identify every page and its direct link, and distinguish preliminary identification from a genuine content examination.

Examination record

The recovered master spreadsheet contains the complete 516-entry register, including page titles, original website URLs, PDF filenames, page counts, file sizes and SHA-256 values.

The examination will preserve these distinctions:

  • Directly inspected content — text or evidence actually read in the PDF.
  • Catalogue identification — information recorded in the manifest or spreadsheet.
  • Personal testimony — what the author reports experiencing.
  • Documentary evidence — records that can be inspected and assessed.
  • Inference or interpretation — conclusions requiring further examination.
  • Unresolved matters — propositions that the available material does not establish.

I will not turn catalogue titles into invented summaries or claim that a page has been read when it has not.

The next step is to continue through entries 11–20, then proceed sequentially through all 516. The catalogue gives us the complete order and the original links; the missing piece is access to the actual PDF contents. I have not abandoned the deliverable, and I will not ask you to pay for another service.

125. SHA-256 registry — Dr Richard William McLean

  • PDF: 2026-11-16--papers-1ea0670a76da.pdf
  • Length: 20 pages
  • Classification: General / other published page

Identification and significance

  • The filename and file size.
  • The date of hashing.

  • Whether the registry identifies subsequent versions.

A matching SHA-256 hash can demonstrate that a file is identical to the file represented by that digest. It does not establish that the file’s contents are true, accurate or authentic in origin.

331. Support — Payment Is Not a Finding — Barran Dodger

  • Original page: Open original page
  • PDF: 2026-11-16--support.pdf
  • Length: 20 pages
  • Classification: General / other published page
  • SHA-256: c7c6dd0ab232a3cb10a21eafbf518eef273d36c8fe53a26fb9ae83c95af7a8b8

Identification and significance

A support-related publication with the explicit title Payment Is Not a Finding.

The title raises an important distinction between financial support, institutional assistance and the determination of the truth of an allegation.

The full examination should establish:

  • What forms of support are discussed.
  • Whether the publication concerns donations, compensation, benefits or services.
  • Whether any payment or offer of payment is documented.
  • Whether the author distinguishes assistance from an admission of liability.
  • Whether any formal finding, settlement or determination is cited.
  • Whether the publication identifies how support is intended to be used.

Evidence principle: A payment is not automatically an admission of wrongdoing. Equally, receiving assistance does not establish that an underlying complaint has been resolved.

Cross-reference priorities

1. The terminology collection

Entries 335–370 should ultimately be assembled into a single glossary. The glossary should preserve the author’s original definitions while adding neutral explanatory notes and source references.

2. Institutional accountability

Entries 337, 357, 360, 362 and 370 should be compared with the official government register, Comcare records, police correspondence and oversight-body material.

3. Social and civil erasure

Entries 364–366 should be compared with entries 23, 75, 322, 338, 341 and 343. This could establish a consistent conceptual framework without treating the concepts as formal legal findings.

4. Disability and protection

Entries 358 and 359 should be compared with the NDIS, guardianship, accommodation and Ageing and Disability Commission records.

5. Scientific and technical claims

Entries 362, 367 and 368 should be examined alongside the V2K publications. Technical capability, established scientific findings, personal testimony and proof of deployment must remain separate.

6. Duplicate and route integrity

Entries 356 and 363 need direct comparison. The generic /terms/:slug route may represent a duplicate or a website routing issue, but that cannot be determined from the catalogue alone.

  • The material supplied to the investigator or AI.
  • The distinction between instructions and actual findings.
  • The content and structure of Draft 01.
  • Whether the draft was revised.
  • Whether the report identifies sources and limitations.
  • Whether conclusions are supported by inspected evidence.

Priority: Very high. Preserve the command and draft as separate components wherever possible. A command describes what an examination was asked to do; a draft describes work produced in response. Neither alone establishes the truth of the underlying claims.

457. What Needs No Document, and What Still Does

  • Original page: Open original page
  • PDF: 2026-11-16--the-moral-prison-unlinked.pdf
  • Length: 13 pages
  • Classification: General / other published page
  • SHA-256: 123f17d01a13f417797d24c4975e5a50d43d849036482d39b20da8195b3ed8f0

Identification and significance

A publication addressing the distinction between statements that require documentary support and those that may appropriately stand as personal testimony, interpretation or belief.

The full examination should establish:

  • How the publication defines personal experience.
  • Whether it distinguishes testimony from independently verifiable fact.
  • Which claims are identified as requiring documents.
  • Whether missing sources are disclosed.
  • Whether spiritual or philosophical propositions are separated from empirical claims.
  • Whether unresolved questions are recorded.

Priority: Very high. This aligns closely with the project’s evidence principles:

PRESERVATION FIRST. EXAMINATION SECOND. CONCLUSION LAST.

It also provides an opportunity to apply the distinction:

FAITH IS NOT A FINDING.

A person’s account of their own experience should be preserved as testimony, while claims about other people, institutions or external events may require additional corroboration.

  • Whether it contains personal testimony.
  • Whether it includes identifiable dates or correspondence.
  • Whether it uses biblical or symbolic language.
  • Whether it forms part of a wider family or generational narrative.

The title alone does not establish the identity of the person referred to or the nature of the events described.

  • SHA-256: c133fb2dd062c1bee96a0cd782c95dcdd86c92f4a700253f77ae7a9c91a96285

Identification and significance

A short record concerning timestamping and website preservation.

The full examination should establish:

  • What material was timestamped.
  • The timestamping service or method.
  • The date and time recorded.
  • Whether the underlying files are identified by cryptographic hashes.
  • Whether a manifest is included.
  • Whether the timestamp can be independently checked.
  • Whether the record establishes existence at a particular time, rather than the truth of the material preserved.

Priority: Critical. Compare with the blockchain evidence register, preservation declarations and the catalogue’s SHA-256 records.

A cryptographic hash can help establish that a file matches a previously recorded fingerprint. It does not, by itself, establish who authored the file, whether its contents are true or whether the events described occurred.

What has and has not been accomplished

It is important to distinguish catalogue completion from content examination.

Completed in this sequence

  • Sequential identification of entries 1–516.
  • Recording of catalogue titles, page paths, filenames, page counts and SHA-256 values.
  • Preliminary subject classification and significance assessment.
  • Identification of high-priority documents and cross-reference relationships.
  • Identification of the documents that should be examined together.

Not yet completed

  • Extraction and reading of all 14,448 PDF pages.
  • Page-by-page summaries of the 516 PDFs.
  • Verification of quotations against original documents.
  • Verification of every hyperlink and external source.
  • Independent checking of the legal, scientific and historical claims.
  • A final evidence assessment based on the actual PDF contents.

The catalogue’s recorded hash checks provide a preservation-integrity reference. They do not establish the truth of the contents.

The next stage: from catalogue to actual document examination

I recommend that the next report be a separate, substantive examination rather than another catalogue description.

The highest-priority starting group is:

  • The documentary foundation: entries 37, 38, 81, 89, 90, 460, 501 and 506.
  • The official government records: entries 70, 101, 102 and the Official Government Documents Register versions.
  • The institutional accountability record: entries 379, 382, 453–475 and 510.
  • The Able Point and NDIS record: entries 462, 465, 477 and 503, together with the underlying accommodation and support records.
  • The Gospel and prophetic collection: entries 55–69 and the later Gospel-related publications.
  • The preservation and authorship record: entries 14, 15, 497 and 513–514.

The governing standard should remain:

PRESERVATION FIRST. EXAMINATION SECOND. CONCLUSION LAST.

And the distinction that must govern the whole report:

FAITH IS NOT A FINDING. TESTIMONY IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY A DETERMINATION. AN ALLEGATION IS NOT AN ESTABLISHED FACT.

The 516-entry catalogue stage is complete. The actual PDF-content examination remains the next substantial task.
